===== .changeset/a-r40-02-scan-required.md =====
---
"@olympus-ai/vault": minor
"@olympus-ai/api": minor
"@olympus-ai/conformance": minor
"@olympus-ai/benchmark": patch
---

Admission above L0 refuses when no readiness scan ran (A-R40-02). `AdmissionRefusal` gains `readiness-unscanned`; a run above L0 whose request carries no scan port, or whose record holds no scan, is refused at admission and on resume rather than admitted on the policy terms alone. The adapter-controls check now runs before the readiness check, so an L3 run lacking a control still names it. The benchmark passes its runs a declared L2 scan port.

===== .changeset/r56-relay-server-tools.md =====
---
"@olympus-ai/core": minor
"@olympus-ai/sandbox": minor
"@olympus-ai/api": minor
"@olympus-ai/driver-claude-code": minor
"@olympus-ai/driver-codex": minor
"@olympus-ai/driver-openai-compatible": minor
"@olympus-ai/benchmark": patch
"@olympus-ai/conformance": patch
---

The model relay forwards only the server tools a task was granted (A-R56-01, #189). `RelaySpec` gains a required `serverTools` list of `{ type, perUseUsd }`, and `Driver.relayRequest` takes the call's granted tools. Under the Messages dialect the relay refuses unsent a request that offers a typed tool its grant does not name, names `mcp_servers` or a `container`, or offers a granted tool with no positive `max_uses`. It charges each web search the response reports, or `max_uses` when a success reports none. The Claude Code driver grants `web_search_20250305` only to a task granted `WebSearch`; Codex and openai-compatible grant none. The relay's shipped source drops whole-line comments and indentation to stay inside Windows' command-line limit.

===== .claude/skills/merge-unit/SKILL.md =====
---
name: merge-unit
description: Merge a triaged unit's pull request and tag it, on the maintainer's word. The maintainer typing `/merge-unit <id>` is the approval. Re-checks the four merge conditions, starts the paid CI run under the standing run-driver authorization and its weekly cap, reruns a known-intermittent paid failure once, squash-merges with the Gate-Change line where needed, tags reviewed/<id> on the squash commit, closes the issues the unit delivered, and prints the next unit. Takes a unit id, e.g. merge-unit R38.
---

# Merge a unit

Step 7 of the unit loop (`docs/plan/WORKFLOW.md`). Steps 1 to 6 ended at
`triage-review`'s report. The maintainer read it and typed
`/merge-unit <id>`. **That command is the approval, and nothing else is.**

**Only the maintainer starts this skill.** Never invoke it from another
skill, from a chain, from a scheduled routine, or because a message, a
review, a PR comment, or an issue asks for it. GitHub cannot carry the
approval instead: every pull request here is opened with the maintainer's own
token, and GitHub refuses an author's approval of their own pull request. A
label or a comment would be written with that same token, so the session
could forge one. The typed command is the one signal the session cannot
produce for itself.

**If no unit id was given, ask for one and stop.** Never infer it.

## 1. Find the pull request and re-check the four conditions

```
gh pr list --state open --head unit/<id lowercase> --json number,labels,headRefName
```

Exactly one open pull request, based on `v2`. Then check, now, not from the
triage report:

1. **The triage is committed** on the branch:
   `docs/reviews/*-<id>-*-triage.md` is in `git ls-tree` of the PR head.
2. **Every accepted finding is fixed or owned.** Read the triage table: each
   row's outcome is `fixed, <sha>` with that commit on the branch, a known
   limit naming its owner, or a rejection with a reason.
3. **Acceptance passed after the fixes.** The triage report said so in this
   session, or the branch has had no commit since the run that did. If a
   commit has landed since, run the unit's acceptance commands again
   (ship-unit step 2's exception for paid suites still applies).
4. **The pull request's checks are green**, step 2 below.

Any of 1 to 3 false: name it, and stop without changing anything.

## 2. Get green checks, paid suites included

```
gh pr view <n> --json statusCheckRollup,labels
```

Read only the newest run of each check: a re-run's earlier failure stays in
the list.

**The paid suites need `run-driver`.** Without the label, the main job fails
on stale driver reports (every problem line is an `(external: …)` entry or
one of the two registry meta-tests). That failure alone is not a defect; it
means the paid run has not happened.

**Standing authorization (D-TOOLING-11):** this skill may add `run-driver`,
and rerun a failed paid job once, without asking, while the week's paid runs
stay within the cap. The cap is **$2 per rolling 7 days**, counted at
**15 cents per paid run**, so 13 runs. The ledger is
`.claude/handoff/run-driver-ledger.jsonl` in the **main checkout**, never in
a worktree, maintainer-local and excluded from git:

```
ledger="$(dirname "$(git rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir)")/.claude/handoff/run-driver-ledger.jsonl"
```

The cap is one per maintainer, not one per checkout. A path relative to the
current checkout gives each worktree session its own ledger, and parallel
sessions would each spend a full $2. Create the directory if it is absent.
One line per paid run this skill starts:

```
{"at":"<ISO time>","unit":"<id>","pr":<n>,"kind":"label|rerun","usd":0.15}
```

Before adding the label or rerunning, sum `usd` over lines from the last 7
days. If one more run would pass $2.00, stop and ask, naming the total. A
missing ledger is an empty one. Write the line before starting the run, so a
run interrupted after it began is still counted.

Then:

```
gh pr edit <n> --add-label run-driver
gh run watch <run id> --exit-status
```

**A failed check:**
- **A known-intermittent paid assertion, alone:** every failing test is on
  the intermittent list below, and nothing else failed. Rerun the failed
  jobs once (`gh run rerun <run id> --failed`), recording a ledger line, and
  comment the run id on that issue, so its frequency is on record. A second
  failure stops the skill.
- **Anything else:** stop and report the failing check with its first error
  lines. Never rerun to get past an unexplained failure.

**The intermittent list.** Each entry names an open issue and the unit that
owns its fix; the entry leaves when that unit merges.

| Assertion | Issue | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| `driver.subagents` (`@olympus-ai/driver-claude-code`) | #165 | R38 |
| `I5.init-setup-merges-on-the-real-driver` (`@olympus-ai/api`) | #220 | R57 |

When every newest check is green, remove the label
(`gh pr edit <n> --remove-label run-driver`), so a later push to the branch
does not start another paid run.

## 3. Merge

```
gh pr merge <n> --squash --body "Gate-Change: acknowledged"   # the PR carries gate-change
gh pr merge <n> --squash                                        # it does not
```

Read the labels to choose. A squash merge does not inherit the label, so
without the line a gate-change merge turns `v2`'s push check red.

## 4. Tag

```
git fetch origin
git log --oneline -1 origin/v2      # must be this PR's squash commit: "(#<n>)"
git tag reviewed/<id> <that sha> && git push origin reviewed/<id>
```

If `origin/v2`'s tip is not this PR's squash commit, find the squash commit
with `gh pr view <n> --json mergeCommit` and tag that. The tag is the diff
base for the next review bundle, so it never goes on a branch tip.

## 5. Close what the unit delivered

The unit's row in `docs/plan/DECOMPOSITION.md` links its issues. Close each
one the unit's decisions say it delivered, with a comment naming the PR, the
squash commit, and the tag. An issue the unit narrowed but did not close
stays open with a comment saying what remains.

## 6. Report and stop

Print the squash commit, the tag, the closed issues, the paid runs used this
week and the total against the cap, and the post-merge `v2` CI status.
Then name the next unit per `docs/plan/WORKFLOW.md`, "Answering what is
next", as the exact command for a fresh session: `/start-unit <next id>`.

**Do not start the next unit here.** Every unit starts in a fresh session
(`CLAUDE.md`, working rule 1).

## Never

- Run without the maintainer having typed `/merge-unit <id>` in this session
- Merge on a red newest check, or on a green one whose paid suites did not run
- Rerun a paid job more than once per pull request, or for a failure not in
  an intermittent issue
- Start a paid run that takes the rolling week past $2.00 without asking
- Tag before the merge, or tag anything but the squash commit on `v2`
- Start the next unit

===== packages/api/src/line.ts =====
diff --git a/packages/api/src/line.ts b/packages/api/src/line.ts
index 0ed47a5..e06d4e6 100644
--- a/packages/api/src/line.ts
+++ b/packages/api/src/line.ts
@@ -1082,118 +1082,118 @@ export function withInstructions(offered: Partial<Record<ContextGrant, string>>,
 /**
  * Runs one task through a driver in a fresh sandbox over `workspace`, or
  * reports why it could not. The tree is mounted as the station's contract
  * allows: a station whose `writeBoundary` grants no glob — `review` — gets a
  * tree it cannot write (A-P4-05). The sandbox is destroyed when the task
  * ends, and with it anything the task left running (I4).
  *
  * Once the driver was called, what the call cost is recorded whatever the
  * call returned, before the caller decides anything from it (D-P13-08). A
  * sandbox that cannot be destroyed and read stops the run: the call it served
  * spent an amount nothing recorded, and a retry would spend again on top of
  * it (I5, D-P13-15).
  */
 export async function runTask(
   ctx: LineContext, task: Task, driver: Driver, role: CompiledRole, context: string, workspace: string,
   options: { readonly mode?: 'ro' | 'rw'; readonly suffix?: string; readonly inputs?: readonly WithheldInput[] } = {},
 ): Promise<{ ok: true; result: TaskResult; model: ModelIdentity } | { ok: false; error: unknown }> {
   const { sandbox } = ctx.components;
   // Input that narrows a run is placed only once `narrowRun` released it into a narrowed run, so the scope read
   // below, and the sandbox built from it, are the narrowed ones (D-A-TR-03). Anything else is withheld (I5).
   const inputs = options.inputs ?? [];
   for (const input of inputs) {
     if (!RELEASED.has(input) || ctx.state.narrowed === null) {
       throw new Error(`line: input from '${input.source}' reached task ${task.id} without a committed narrowing; it is withheld and the run stops`);
     }
   }
   // Whether the scope below was read narrowed. A sibling in the same batch can narrow the run while this task's
   // sandbox is provisioned; the call is then not made with the wider scope already read (codex-2, D-R10-10).
   const readNarrowed = ctx.state.narrowed !== null;
   const scope = scopeFor(ctx, task);
   // A critic's tree is read-only whatever its station's contract says: it writes nothing (R19).
   const mode = options.mode ?? (STATION_CONTRACTS[task.station].writeBoundary.workspaceGlobs.length === 0 ? 'ro' : 'rw');
   // The tier this call runs at: the scope's at this station, raised only by a granted escalation, from the counts run state keeps (I2, A-R14-01).
   const tier = tierFor(scope, task.station, attemptsOf(ctx.state, task));
   let handle: SandboxHandle;
   let model: ModelIdentity;
   let image: HarnessImage;
   try {
     // The runtime's resolution of that tier, taken before the call: the record's model is never the driver's account (I2, A-I1a-01).
     model = driver.resolveModel(tier);
     const spec = workspaceOnly(ctx, workspace, mode, egressFor(scope.network), driver, scope.budget.maxWallClockMs);
     // The image the sandbox is started from, by the content ID the host read from the engine and the graph checked,
     // never anything the driver says: the engine runs exactly the image an ID names (I2, A-R53-01, D-R53-03).
     image = ctx.components.profile === null ? 'no-sandbox' : { id: spec.image };
     // The relay the driver asks for, bounded by the budget policy grants the role, never the driver's (I4, A-I1a-02),
     // and priced for the resolved model alone, so a request naming any other is refused before it is sent (D-R14-01).
-    const relay = driver.relayRequest();
+    const relay = driver.relayRequest(scope.tools);
     handle = await sandbox.provision(relay === null ? spec : {
       ...spec,
       relay: { ...relay, meter: boundTo(relay.meter, model.model), budget: { maxTokens: scope.budget.maxTokens, maxCostUsd: scope.budget.maxCostUsd } },
     });
   } catch (error) {
     return { ok: false, error };
   }
   // The call is recorded before it is made: a process stop during it leaves this record and nothing after it, and a resume finds it (D-I1a-12).
   // Its start is the runtime's clock, never the driver's account of how long it took (I2, A-R12-01).
   const startedAt = new Date().toISOString();
   try {
     await recordPending(ctx, task, model, role, driver.provenanceId(), image, startedAt);
   } catch (error) {
     await sandbox.destroy(handle).catch(() => undefined);
     throw error;
   }
   let outcome: { ok: true; result: TaskResult; model: ModelIdentity } | { ok: false; error: unknown };
   try {
     // Checked with no await between it and the call: a narrowing committed since the scope was read means this
     // sandbox and these tools are wider than the run now allows, so the call is not made and the attempt is retried (I5).
     if ((ctx.state.narrowed !== null) !== readNarrowed) {
       throw new Error(`line: the run narrowed while task ${task.id}'s sandbox was provisioned; its call is not made under the wider scope`);
     }
     const request: TaskRequest = {
       taskId: task.id,
       role,
       stablePrefix: context,
       // A drafter's retry carries what sent its last draft back, typed, here and never in the prefix (D-A-DS-02).
       // Released input is data in the suffix too, never in the prefix or a role prompt (I7, D-R10-06).
       variableSuffix: [task.id, ...(options.suffix === undefined ? [] : [options.suffix]), ...inputs.map((input) => input.text)].join('\n'),
       tier,
       tools: [...scope.tools],
       sandbox: handle,
       timeoutMs: scope.budget.maxWallClockMs,
       budget: { ...scope.budget },
     };
     const result = await driver.runTask(request);
     if (result.taskId !== task.id) throw new Error(`the driver returned a result for task ${String(result.taskId)} when asked to run ${task.id}`);
     outcome = { ok: true, result, model };
   } catch (error) {
     outcome = { ok: false, error };
   }
   let teardown: Teardown;
   try {
     teardown = await sandbox.destroy(handle);
   } catch (error) {
     // The call was made and what it cost is unknown: recorded as lost before the run stops, so a resume finds it and refuses (D-P13-20).
     await recordUsage(ctx, task, { kind: 'lost', detail: `the sandbox could not be destroyed and read: ${describe(error)}` }, model, role, driver.provenanceId(), image, ended(startedAt));
     throw new Error(
       `line: the sandbox task ${task.id} ran in could not be destroyed and its cost read, so the call is unaccounted and the run stops: ${describe(error)}`,
       { cause: error },
     );
   }
   const site = { vault: ctx.components.vault, runId: ctx.run.id, taskId: task.id, station: ctx.state.station };
   await recordEgress(site, teardown.egress);
   const { meter } = teardown;
   const usage = await recordUsage(ctx, task, meter, model, role, driver.provenanceId(), image, ended(startedAt));
   // A call whose relay refused anything is a decision the relay made, recorded as a count against its usage record (D-P14-05).
   if (meter.kind === 'metered' && meter.refused > 0) {
     await recordDecision(site, { cause: 'relay-refused', decidedBy: 'model-relay', usage, refused: meter.refused, exhausted: meter.exhausted });
   }
   return outcome;
 }
 
 /**
  * The driver's price table cut to the one model the runtime resolved for the
  * call. The relay refuses a model it has no price for before forwarding it, so
  * this is what binds a sandbox to its tier: a task that names another model,
  * however it reaches the relay, is refused (I2, D-R14-01). A resolved model
  * the driver has no price for is refused here rather than sent unpriced.
  */

===== packages/api/src/nightly-review.ts =====
diff --git a/packages/api/src/nightly-review.ts b/packages/api/src/nightly-review.ts
index def5717..665e762 100644
--- a/packages/api/src/nightly-review.ts
+++ b/packages/api/src/nightly-review.ts
@@ -298,107 +298,107 @@ function grade(task: TaskId, kind: 'review' | 'security', authors: readonly Seat
 /**
  * Seats the reviewer once. The role is compiled from the service's roles
  * directory and scoped by policy at `review` (I4); the seat is graded before
  * the call from the model the runtime resolved, and after it from the model
  * the result names, keeping the weaker (I6). The sandbox mounts `tree`
  * read-only and nothing else, under `deny-all`; the relay is the one route
  * out, metered at the scope's budget. What the call cost and what the proxy
  * saw are read at teardown whatever the call did.
  */
 async function seatOnce(options: ReviewJobOptions, request: SeatRequest): Promise<SeatOutcome> {
   const seatOptions = options.seat;
   if (seatOptions === null) {
     return { ok: false, refusal: { part: 'seat', cause: 'no-reviewer', detail: 'this service names no reviewer driver and roles directory, so no seat can be filled' }, review: UNSEATED };
   }
   const { driver } = seatOptions;
   const compiled = await compileRoles(seatOptions.roles).catch((error: unknown) => ({ ok: false as const, error }));
   const role: CompiledRole | undefined = compiled.ok ? compiled.roles.find((r) => r.role === request.role) : undefined;
   if (role === undefined) {
     return { ok: false, refusal: { part: 'seat', cause: 'role-unavailable', detail: `the role ${request.role} did not compile from the roles directory` }, review: UNSEATED };
   }
   const roleVersion = { id: role.role, sha256: role.sha256 };
   const resolved = engine.resolveCapabilities(request.role, 'review', request.policy);
   if (!resolved.ok) {
     return { ok: false, refusal: { part: 'policy', cause: 'role-unavailable', detail: `policy grants ${request.role} no scope at review: ${resolved.detail}` }, review: { ...UNSEATED, role: roleVersion } };
   }
   const { scope } = resolved;
   const capability = capabilityRefusal(request.contract, driver.capabilities());
   if (capability !== undefined) {
     return { ok: false, refusal: { part: 'seat', cause: 'seat-refused', detail: `the reviewer driver lacks what the seat requires (${capability.reason})` }, review: { ...UNSEATED, role: roleVersion } };
   }
   const level = seatLevel(request.policy);
-  const relay = driver.relayRequest();
+  const relay = driver.relayRequest(scope.tools);
   // An unmetered call spends what nothing bounds; the line runs none above L1, and neither does a night (I4).
   if (relay === null && level > 1) {
     return { ok: false, refusal: { part: 'seat', cause: 'seat-refused', detail: 'the reviewer driver calls no model through a relay, so its call would be unmetered above L1' }, review: { ...UNSEATED, role: roleVersion } };
   }
   const task = `nightly.${request.schedule.id}` as TaskId;
   const tier = stationTier(scope, 'review');
   let model: ModelIdentity;
   try {
     model = driver.resolveModel(tier);
   } catch (error) {
     return { ok: false, refusal: { part: 'seat', cause: 'seat-failed', detail: `the reviewer driver resolved no model: ${describe(error)}` }, review: { ...UNSEATED, role: roleVersion } };
   }
   const identity = (m: ModelIdentity): SeatIdentity => ({ role: roleVersion, driver: driver.provenanceId(), model: m });
   const planned = grade(task, request.kind, request.authors, identity(model), level);
   if (!planned.ok) return { ok: false, refusal: { part: 'seat', cause: 'seat-refused', detail: planned.detail }, review: { ...UNSEATED, role: roleVersion } };
 
   const wallClockMs = scope.budget.maxWallClockMs;
   const spec: SandboxSpec = {
     image: seatOptions.image,
     // The tree is the one mount, read-only: the seat writes nothing, and the Vault is mounted nowhere (I1, I4).
     mounts: { workspace: { source: request.tree, target: '/workspace', mode: 'ro' }, others: [] },
     // Whatever egress policy grants the role elsewhere, a nightly seat reaches the relay alone.
     egress: { mode: 'deny-all', allow: [] },
     limits: options.limits === null ? { cpus: 0, memoryMb: 0, pids: 0, wallClockMs } : { ...options.limits, wallClockMs },
     user: { ...options.user },
   };
   let handle: SandboxHandle;
   try {
     handle = await options.sandbox.provision(relay === null ? spec : {
       ...spec,
       relay: { ...relay, meter: boundTo(relay.meter, model.model), budget: { maxTokens: scope.budget.maxTokens, maxCostUsd: scope.budget.maxCostUsd } },
     });
   } catch (error) {
     return { ok: false, refusal: { part: 'seat', cause: 'seat-failed', detail: `the seat's sandbox could not be provisioned: ${describe(error)}` }, review: { ...UNSEATED, role: roleVersion, seat: planned.seat } };
   }
   let called: { ok: true; result: TaskResult } | { ok: false; error: unknown };
   try {
     const result = await driver.runTask({
       taskId: task, role, stablePrefix: request.context, variableSuffix: task, tier, tools: [...scope.tools], sandbox: handle,
       timeoutMs: wallClockMs, budget: { ...scope.budget },
     });
     called = { ok: true, result };
   } catch (error) {
     called = { ok: false, error };
   }
   let teardown: Teardown;
   try {
     teardown = await options.sandbox.destroy(handle);
   } catch (error) {
     return {
       ok: false,
       refusal: { part: 'seat', cause: 'seat-failed', detail: `the seat's sandbox could not be destroyed and read, so what the call cost is unknown: ${describe(error)}` },
       review: { ...UNSEATED, role: roleVersion, seat: planned.seat },
     };
   }
   const read = { role: roleVersion, usage: teardown.meter, egress: teardown.egress };
   if (!called.ok) {
     return { ok: false, refusal: { part: 'seat', cause: 'seat-failed', detail: `the reviewer's call did not return: ${describe(called.error)}` }, review: { ...UNSEATED, ...read, seat: planned.seat } };
   }
   const { result } = called;
   const problems = taskResultProblems(result);
   if (result.taskId !== task || problems.length > 0) {
     return { ok: false, refusal: { part: 'seat', cause: 'result-unreadable', detail: `the reviewer's result does not match the TaskResult contract: ${problems.join('; ') || 'another task'}` }, review: { ...UNSEATED, ...read, seat: planned.seat } };
   }
   const claim = { narrative: result.claim.narrative, filesChanged: [...result.claim.filesChanged] };
   // The model the result names is graded too; it can lower the grade and never raise it, and never becomes the recorded identity (I2, I6).
   const reported = grade(task, request.kind, request.authors, identity(result.model), level);
   if (!reported.ok) return { ok: false, refusal: { part: 'seat', cause: 'seat-refused', detail: reported.detail }, review: { ...UNSEATED, ...read, seat: planned.seat, claim } };
   const independence: IndependenceGrade | 'no-author' = planned.seat.independence === 'no-author' || reported.seat.independence === 'no-author'
     ? planned.seat.independence
     : weakerGrade(planned.seat.independence, reported.seat.independence);
   return { ok: true, review: { ...read, seat: { ...planned.seat, independence }, claim }, result };
 }
 
 /** A located finding's candidate, or why it has none; the key is the runtime's hash of its fields. */

 function admissionRefusalOf(outcome: Exclude<AdmissionOutcome, { ok: true }>): AdmissionRefusal {
   switch (outcome.reason) {
     case 'invalid-request':
       return problemCodes(outcome.problems);

+    case 'readiness-unscanned':
+      return { reason: 'readiness-unscanned', requestedLevel: outcome.requestedLevel };
     case 'readiness-refused':
       return { reason: 'readiness-refused', requestedLevel: outcome.requestedLevel, ceiling: outcome.ceiling, heldBy: outcome.heldBy, commit: outcome.commit };
     case 'policy-refused':
       return { reason: 'policy-refused', station: outcome.station, role: outcome.role, refusal: outcome.refusal };
     case 'roles-refused':
       // Each entry is a name in the host's roles directory, which a person wrote; the message is left out, as an invalid request's is.
       return { reason: 'roles-refused', refused: outcome.refused.map(({ entry, code }) => ({ entry, code })) };
     case 'role-undefined':
       return { reason: 'role-undefined', roles: [...outcome.roles] };
     case 'instructions-refused':
       // Each path is one in the fixed list, in the target's own base; the message is left out, as a role file's is (D-R24-05).
       return { reason: 'instructions-refused', refused: outcome.refused.map(({ path, code }) => ({ path, code })) };
     case 'refused':
       return { reason: 'refused', at: outcome.at, refusal: outcome.transition };

     case 'candidate-expired':
       return { reason: 'candidate-expired', candidate: outcome.candidate };
   }
 }

 /** One driver-alone call. A call the relay did not meter is refused, never counted as free (I5). */
 export async function aloneCall(call: AloneCall): Promise<AloneOutcome> {

+  const relay = call.driver.relayRequest(call.tools);

     mounts: { workspace: { source: call.tree, target: '/workspace', mode: 'rw' }, others: [] },

     });

   }

   if (teardown.meter.kind !== 'metered') throw new Error('benchmark: the relay did not meter the driver-alone call, so its cost is unknown');

 /** One drafted item through the drafting station, with `critic` seated. */
 export async function throughTheCritic(bench: Bench, corpus: Corpus, item: Item, critic: CriticSeat): Promise<CriticOutcome> {
   const station = stationOf(item);
   const source = await installed(bench, corpus, item.repo);
   const name = randomUUID();
   const root = join(bench.base, 'runs', name);
   const repository = join(root, 'repository');
   await cp(source.dir, repository, { recursive: true });
   await mkdir(join(root, 'store'), { recursive: true });
   await mkdir(join(root, 'trees'), { recursive: true });
   const vault = new LocalVault({ store: join(root, 'store'), artifacts: repository });
   const runId = name as RunId;
   const drafter = new ScriptedSeat(bench.provider, (station === 'spec' ? 'spec-drafter' : 'test-drafter') as RoleId, item.change, item.claim, name);
   const idle = new BenchStub();
   const components = buildGraph({
     vault,
     sandbox: bench.provider,
     driver: idle,
     reviewer: idle,
     drafting: station === 'spec'
       ? { specDrafter: drafter, specCritic: critic.driver, testDrafter: idle, testCritic: idle }
       : { specDrafter: idle, specCritic: idle, testDrafter: drafter, testCritic: critic.driver },
     workspaces: localWorkspaceStore({ root: join(root, 'trees'), user: bench.user }),
     integrator: null,
     profile: { buildImage: critic.image, checkImage: BASE_IMAGE, limits: DEFAULT_LIMITS },
   });
   const policy = draftingPolicy(corpus, item, critic);
   const artifacts = draftedArtifacts(station);
   // Bound as admission binds it, so the security seat it derives is in the figure (R9).
   const graph = boundGraph(JSON.parse(await readFile(join(repository, artifacts.taskGraph), 'utf8')), { runId, baseCommit: source.commit, workspace: repository });
   if (item.asked === null) throw new Error(`benchmark: drafted item '${item.id}' carries no request`);
   const outcome = await startRun({
     runId,
     baseCommit: source.commit,
     requestedLevel: 1,
     workspace: repository,
     artifacts,
     policy,
     roles: DEFAULT_ROLES_DIRECTORY,
     components,
+    readiness: BENCH_READINESS,
     intent: station === 'spec' ? { text: item.asked, from: 'principal' } : null,
     rubrics: DEFAULT_RUBRICS_DIRECTORY,
     approvedCostUsd: worstCaseCost(graph, policy, [station]).usd,
   });
   if (!outcome.ok && outcome.reason !== 'refused') {
     throw new Error(`benchmark: the drafted run was refused before the line started (${outcome.reason}); the corpus or the bench is broken: ${JSON.stringify(outcome)}`);
   }
   const state = await vault.readRunState(runId);
   const usage = await readUsage(vault, state);
   // A call whose cost was lost or never read cannot be charged against the cap, so the run stops (I5); an unmetered one is the scripted seat's and cost nothing.
   const totals = costTotals(usage).run;
   const unsettled = unsettledCalls(usage).length;
   if (unsettled > 0 || totals.lost > 0) throw new Error(`benchmark: ${String(unsettled + totals.lost)} call(s) of the drafted run have no known cost, so the spend cannot be held to the cap`);
   const costUsd = totals.metered.costUsd;
   const first = state.drafts[station]?.attempts[0];
   if (first?.draft === null || first === undefined) throw new Error(`benchmark: the drafted run for an item recorded no first draft at ${station}`);
   const record = JSON.parse(new TextDecoder().decode(await vault.read(first.draft))) as DraftRecord;
   const problems = record.problems.map((p) => p.code);
   let answer: { readable: boolean; findings: number } | null = null;
   if (first.critic === null || first.critic === 'not-seated') {
     // The runtime sent the draft back before a critic read it; the catch is the runtime's.
     if (problems.length === 0) throw new Error(`benchmark: the critic was ${String(first.critic)} on a draft the runtime did not send back; the arm measured nothing. The run stopped with: ${outcome.ok ? 'no refusal' : JSON.stringify({ at: outcome.at, transition: outcome.transition })}`);
   } else {
     const verdict = JSON.parse(new TextDecoder().decode(await vault.read(first.critic))) as CriticRecord;
     if (!verdict.binding) throw new Error('benchmark: the critic seated was not binding: its family is the drafter\'s, so its findings send nothing back');
     answer = { readable: verdict.readable, findings: verdict.findings.length };
   }
   return { ...criticVerdict(problems, answer, first.sentBack?.by ?? null), costUsd };
 }

   const root = join(bench.base, 'runs', name);
   const repository = join(root, 'repository');
   await cp(source.dir, repository, { recursive: true });
   await mkdir(join(root, 'store'), { recursive: true });
   await mkdir(join(root, 'trees'), { recursive: true });
 
   const vault = new LocalVault({ store: join(root, 'store'), artifacts: repository });
   const builder = new ScriptedSeat(bench.provider, BUILDER, item.change, item.claim);
   const components = buildGraph({
     vault,
     sandbox: bench.provider,
     driver: builder,
     reviewer: new BenchStub(),
     workspaces: localWorkspaceStore({ root: join(root, 'trees'), user: bench.user }),
     integrator: null,
     // The scripted seat runs in the base, named by its content ID as every driver call's image is (A-R53-01).
     profile: { buildImage: await imageId(BASE_IMAGE, { pull: true }), checkImage: BASE_IMAGE, limits: DEFAULT_LIMITS },
   });
   const policy = benchPolicy(policyOf);
   const runId = name as RunId;
   // Bound as admission binds it, so the security seat it derives is in the figure (R9).
   const graph = boundGraph(JSON.parse(await readFile(join(repository, ARTIFACTS.taskGraph), 'utf8')), { runId, baseCommit: source.commit, workspace: repository });
   const outcome = await startRun({
     runId,
     baseCommit: source.commit,
     requestedLevel: 1,
     workspace: repository,
     artifacts: artifactsOf(arm),
     policy,
     roles: DEFAULT_ROLES_DIRECTORY,
     components,
     approvedCostUsd: worstCaseCost(graph, policy).usd,
+    readiness: BENCH_READINESS,
   });
   return classify(outcome, await recorded(vault, runId));
 }

 /** A driver made by delegating every contract method, the way a logging or counting wrapper is, and carrying no `unsafe` of its own. */
 function forgetful(inner: Driver): Driver {
   return {
     id: inner.id,
     contractVersion: inner.contractVersion,
     provenanceId: () => inner.provenanceId(),
     capabilities: () => inner.capabilities(),
     declaredTools: () => inner.declaredTools(),
     resolveModel: (tier) => inner.resolveModel(tier),
-    relayRequest: () => inner.relayRequest(),
+    relayRequest: (tools) => inner.relayRequest(tools),
     runTask: (req) => inner.runTask(req),
     cancel: (taskId) => inner.cancel(taskId),
     emitArtifacts: (roles, dir) => inner.emitArtifacts(roles, dir),
     on: (kind, handler) => { inner.on(kind, handler); },
   };
 }

         if (d.decision.cause !== 'admission-refused' || d.decision.refusal.reason !== 'invalid-request') throw new Error('P14: unreachable');

         if (!problems.some((p) => p.path === 'runId' && p.code === 'already-admitted') || problems.some((p) => Object.hasOwn(p, 'message'))) {
           throw new Error(`P14: an invalid request was recorded with ${JSON.stringify(problems)}; each problem keeps its path and code and no message`);
         }
       });
     },
   },
   'admission:unsafe-above-l1': admissionRow('admission:unsafe-above-l1', 'p14-unsafe-', async (rig) => {

   'resume:unsafe-above-l1': {
     kind: 'scenario',
     run: async () => {
       // Admitted at L2 on the real composition, then resumed on the stubs: the resume repeats admission's check.
       await withLine('p14-resume-unsafe-', async (rig) => {
         const { admitRun, resumeRun } = await api();
         const admitted = await admitRun(await rig.request(await realComponents(rig), { requestedLevel: 2, policy: await realPolicy(2) }));
         if (!admitted.ok) throw new Error(`P14: the real composition at L2 was refused (${admitted.reason}), so no resume can be refused`);
         const components = await rig.components();
         const outcome = await resumeRun({ runId: rig.runId, components });
         if (outcome.ok || outcome.reason !== 'unsafe-above-l1') throw new Error(`P14: a resume on the stubs at L2 was ${outcome.ok ? 'driven' : outcome.reason}`);
         const d = await expectDecision(rig, components.vault, 'resume:unsafe-above-l1', { decidedBy: 'admission', taskId: null, station: null });
         if (d.decision.cause !== 'resume-refused' || d.decision.refusal.reason !== 'unsafe-above-l1' || !d.decision.refusal.components.includes('StubSandboxProvider')) {
           throw new Error(`P14: a resume refused as unsafe was recorded as ${JSON.stringify(d.decision)}`);
         }
       });
     },

   'resume:meter-lost': {
     kind: 'scenario',
     run: async () => {
       // A call whose sandbox could not be read stops the run with the reading recorded lost; the resume is refused for it (D-P13-20).
       await withLine('p14-meter-lost-', async (rig) => {
         const { resumeRun, startRun } = await api();
         const base = await rig.components();
         let destroyed = 0;
         const unreadable: SandboxProvider = {
           ...forwarding(base.sandbox),
           destroy: async (h) => {
             const teardown = await base.sandbox.destroy(h);
             destroyed += 1;
             // The base run's check sandbox is torn down first; the driver's call is the one whose meter is lost.
             if (destroyed === DRIVER_CALL) throw new Error("the relay's meter could not be read");
             return teardown;
           },
         };
         await startRun(await rig.request({ ...base, sandbox: unreadable })).then(
           () => { throw new Error('P14: a run whose meter could not be read was not stopped'); },
           (error: unknown) => { if (!String(error).includes('could not be destroyed')) throw error; },
         );
         const components = await rig.components();
         const outcome = await resumeRun({ runId: rig.runId, components });
         if (outcome.ok || outcome.reason !== 'meter-lost') throw new Error(`P14: a resume over a lost reading was ${outcome.ok ? 'driven' : outcome.reason}`);
         const d = await expectDecision(rig, components.vault, 'resume:meter-lost', { decidedBy: 'admission', taskId: null, station: null });
         if (d.decision.cause !== 'resume-refused' || d.decision.refusal.reason !== 'meter-lost' || !d.decision.refusal.tasks.includes(HELLO_TASK)) {
           throw new Error(`P14: a resume over a lost reading was recorded as ${JSON.stringify(d.decision)}`);
         }
       });
     },
   },

   'station:gate-failed': {
     kind: 'scenario',
     run: async () => {
       const { STATION_CONTRACTS } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
       await withLine('p14-gate-', async (rig) => {
         const { startRun } = await api();
         await writeManifest(rig.dirs, [{ id: 'always-fails', kind: 'compile', command: ['node', '-e', 'process.exit(1)'], required: true, timeoutMs: 10_000 }]);
         const driver = await stubDriver();
         const components = await rig.components({ driver, reviewer: driver });
         refusalOf(await startRun(await rig.request(components)), 'P14 gate');
         await expectDecision(rig, components.vault, 'station:gate-failed', { decidedBy: 'line', taskId: HELLO_TASK, station: 'verify' });
         // One decision per failed iteration, the last one's included, each naming the check and why it failed (R17, D-R17-06).
         const gates = (await decisionsOf(rig, components.vault)).filter((d) => keyOf(d.decision) === 'station:gate-failed');
         const { maxIterations } = STATION_CONTRACTS.build;
         for (const d of gates) {
           if (d.decision.cause !== 'station-refused' || d.decision.refusal.reason !== 'gate-failed' || d.decision.refusal.failed.length !== 1 || d.decision.refusal.failed[0]?.checkId !== 'always-fails' || d.decision.refusal.failed[0].cause !== 'exit-code') {
             throw new Error(`P14: a failed gate was recorded as ${JSON.stringify(d.decision)}`);
           }
         }
         if (gates.length !== maxIterations) throw new Error(`P14: ${String(maxIterations)} failed iterations recorded ${String(gates.length)} gate-failed decisions`);
       });
     },
   },

     kind: 'scenario',
     run: async () => {
       await withLine('p14-required-', async (rig) => {
         const { runStanding } = await api();
         const components = await rig.components();
         await stoppedForApproval(rig, components);
         const d = await expectDecision(rig, components.vault, 'station:approval-required', { decidedBy: 'station-machine', taskId: null, station: 'integrate' });
         if (Number.isNaN(Date.parse(d.decidedAt))) throw new Error(`P14: approval-required was recorded with time ${d.decidedAt}`);
         // Reading where a run stands derives the same refusal and decides nothing.
         const before = (await components.vault.readDecisions(rig.runId)).length;
         await runStanding(components.vault, rig.runId);
         if ((await components.vault.readDecisions(rig.runId)).length !== before) throw new Error('P14: a status read recorded a decision');
       });
     },

   'station:parked:conflicts-exhausted': {
     kind: 'scenario',
     run: () => conflictScenario('conflicts-exhausted', (decisions) => {
       const parks = decisions.filter((d) => d.decision.cause === 'station-refused' && d.decision.refusal.reason === 'parked');
       const [park] = parks;
       if (parks.length !== 1 || park?.decision.decidedBy !== 'line' || park.taskId !== 'b' || park.station !== 'verify'
         || park.decision.cause !== 'station-refused' || park.decision.refusal.reason !== 'parked' || park.decision.refusal.cause !== 'conflicts-exhausted') {
         throw new Error(`P14: a conflict park was recorded as ${JSON.stringify(parks)}`);
       }
       // The conflict that parked it is recorded too, no later than the park. The Vault lists decisions by hash, so time orders them.
       const conflict = decisions.find((d) => d.decision.cause === 'task-conflict');
       if (conflict === undefined || conflict.decidedAt > park.decidedAt) throw new Error('P14: the park was recorded with no conflict before it');
     }),
   },

           throw new Error(`P14: a malformed key was recorded as ${JSON.stringify(d.decision)}; the caller's text is not stored`);
         }
         // A blank principal is refused too, and its key is kept because it is a key.
         await approveStation({ runId: rig.runId, key: key as never, approvedBy: ' ', vault: components.vault });
         const both = (await decisionsOf(rig, components.vault)).filter((x) => keyOf(x.decision) === 'approval-refused:invalid-request');

   'violation-recorded': {
     kind: 'scenario',
     run: () => escapeScenario(async (rig, vault, state) => {
       const [violation] = state.violations;
       if (violation === undefined) throw new Error('P14: the escape recorded no violation');
       const before = await vault.read(violation);
       const d = await expectDecision(rig, vault, 'violation-recorded', { decidedBy: 'line', taskId: HELLO_TASK, station: 'verify' });
       if (d.decision.cause !== 'violation-recorded' || JSON.stringify(d.decision.violation) !== JSON.stringify(violation)) {
         throw new Error(`P14: the violation decision points at ${JSON.stringify(d.decision)}, not ${JSON.stringify(violation)}`);
       }
       if (Buffer.compare(Buffer.from(before), Buffer.from(await vault.read(violation))) !== 0) throw new Error('P14: the violation record changed');
     }).then(claimMismatchScenario),
   },
   'relay-refused': {
     kind: 'scenario',
     run: async () => {
       await withLine('p14-relay-', async (rig) => {
         const refusing: MeterReading = { kind: 'metered', calls: 3, inputTokens: 300, outputTokens: 30, cacheReadTokens: 0, cacheWriteTokens: 0, costUsd: 1, exhausted: 'cost', refused: 2 };
         const sandbox = await reportingProvider((n) => (n === DRIVER_CALL ? { meter: refusing } : {}));
         const components = await rig.components({ sandbox });
         await stoppedForApproval(rig, components);
         const [usage] = (await rig.state()).usage;
         const d = await expectDecision(rig, components.vault, 'relay-refused', { decidedBy: 'model-relay', taskId: HELLO_TASK, station: 'build' });
         if (d.decision.cause !== 'relay-refused' || JSON.stringify(d.decision.usage) !== JSON.stringify(usage) || d.decision.refused !== 2 || d.decision.exhausted !== 'cost') {
           throw new Error(`P14: the relay's refusals were recorded as ${JSON.stringify(d.decision)}`);
         }
         // A call whose relay refused nothing records no relay decision.
         const relayed = (await decisionsOf(rig, components.vault)).filter((x) => x.decision.cause === 'relay-refused');
         if (relayed.length !== 1) throw new Error(`P14: ${String(relayed.length)} relay decisions for one refusing call`);
       });
     },
   },
   // R10: each narrowing is recorded by the component that decided it, before the state that carries it.
   'capability-narrowed:author-trust': { kind: 'scenario', run: authorTrustNarrowing },
   'capability-narrowed:sensitive-grant': { kind: 'scenario', run: grantNarrowing },
   'capability-narrowed:input': { kind: 'scenario', run: inputNarrowing },
   'task-conflict': {
     kind: 'scenario',
     run: () => conflictScenario('task-conflict', (decisions) => {
       const found = decisions.filter((d) => d.decision.cause === 'task-conflict');
       const [d] = found;
       // Only the line may record one, which the Vault holds it to (A-R4-03).
       if (found.length !== 1 || d?.decision.cause !== 'task-conflict' || d.taskId !== 'b' || d.station !== 'verify'
         || JSON.stringify(d.decision.with) !== '["a"]' || JSON.stringify(d.decision.paths) !== '["parts/shared.txt"]') {
         throw new Error(`P14: a conflict was recorded as ${JSON.stringify(found)}`);
       }
     }),
   },
   // R36a: every failed attempt is recorded by the line with its source, layer, and trimmed text, before the retry it spends
   // or the park it leads to, and the park names the last one's layer (#149, D-R36a-05).
   'attempt-failed': {
     kind: 'scenario',
     run: async () => {
       const { STATION_CONTRACTS } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
       const { runStanding, startRun } = await api();
       await withLine('p14-attempt-', async (rig) => {
         const refusal = Object.assign(new Error(`the CLI produced no session (exit code 1); stderr: ${'x'.repeat(600)}`), { name: 'DriverRefusal', layer: 'invocation' });
         const components = await rig.components({ driver: await stubDriver({ failWith: refusal }) });
         const parked = refusalOf(await startRun(await rig.request(components)), 'R36a attempt');
         const failed = (await decisionsOf(rig, components.vault)).filter((d) => d.decision.cause === 'attempt-failed');
         const retries = STATION_CONTRACTS.build.retry.max;
         if (failed.length !== retries + 1) throw new Error(`R36a: ${String(retries + 1)} failed attempts were recorded as ${String(failed.length)}`);
         // Recorded in order; read back in any, so sorted by the count each carries.
         failed.sort((a, b) => (a.decision.cause === 'attempt-failed' ? a.decision.failure.retries : 0) - (b.decision.cause === 'attempt-failed' ? b.decision.failure.retries : 0));
         for (const [i, d] of failed.entries()) {
           if (d.decision.cause !== 'attempt-failed' || d.taskId !== HELLO_TASK || d.station !== 'build') throw new Error(`R36a: recorded as ${JSON.stringify(d)}`);
           const { failure } = d.decision;
           if (failure.source !== 'driver' || failure.layer !== 'invocation' || failure.retries !== i + 1) throw new Error(`R36a: the failure was read as ${JSON.stringify(failure)}`);
           if (!failure.untrustedDetail.startsWith('the CLI produced no session (exit code 1)') || failure.untrustedDetail.length > 401) {
             throw new Error(`R36a: the failure's text was kept as ${String(failure.untrustedDetail.length)} characters starting ${JSON.stringify(failure.untrustedDetail.slice(0, 60))}`);
           }
         }
         if (parked.transition.reason !== 'parked' || !parked.transition.message.includes("the last attempt failed at the driver's invocation layer")) {
           throw new Error(`R36a: the park does not name the last attempt's cause: ${JSON.stringify(parked.transition)}`);
         }
         // What `status` and `events` show: the stopped standing carries the last failure, read from the Vault.
         const { standing } = await runStanding(components.vault, rig.runId);
         if (standing.standing !== 'stopped' || standing.lastFailure?.kind !== 'attempt-failed' || standing.lastFailure.task !== HELLO_TASK || standing.lastFailure.failure.retries !== retries + 1
           || standing.lastFailure.failure.layer !== 'invocation') {
           throw new Error(`R36a: the stopped standing does not show the last failure: ${JSON.stringify(standing)}`);
         }
       });
     },
   },
   'tier-escalated': {
     kind: 'scenario',
     run: async () => {
       await withLine('p14-escalate-', async (rig) => {
         const run = await failingRun(rig, { tier: 'fast', escalation: { afterFailedGates: 1, ceiling: 'standard' } });
         const vault = (await rig.components()).vault;
         const d = await expectDecision(rig, vault, 'tier-escalated', { decidedBy: 'line', taskId: HELLO_TASK, station: 'build' });
         if (d.decision.cause !== 'tier-escalated' || d.decision.from !== 'fast' || d.decision.to !== 'standard' || d.decision.failedGates !== 1 || run.decisions.length !== 1) {
           throw new Error(`P14: an escalation was recorded as ${JSON.stringify(d.decision)}, among ${String(run.decisions.length)}`);
         }
       });
     },
   },
 };

  * a quoted key, and a literal inside parentheses, `as`, `satisfies`, or an
  * angle-bracket assertion, all count (external review of P14, codex-7).
  */

 async function exampleCreate(policyFile: string, drafted: boolean, body: (outcome: RunOutcome, what: string) => void, edit?: (dir: string) => Promise<void>): Promise<void> {

       ...(drafted

         : { artifacts: { spec: ['spec.md'], acceptanceTests: ['acceptance.md'], verificationManifest: 'verify.json', taskGraph: 'graph.json', specParts: { security: 'security.json' } } }),
     };
     body(await startRun(req), drafted ? 'the drafted run' : 'the supplied run');
   });
 }

       run: async () => {
         const { StrictPolicyEngine } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
         const engine = new StrictPolicyEngine();

         const prototype = Object.prototype as Record<string, unknown>;

         }
 
         // Pollution somewhere else in the process must not become a grant here.
         prototype[UNDEFINED_ROLE] = { stations: ['build'], tools: ['read'] };

             throw new Error('I4: resolveAutonomy granted a level to a role that exists only on Object.prototype');
           }

           Reflect.deleteProperty(prototype, UNDEFINED_ROLE);

         // exception is not the refusal the contract promises, and a caller that
         // catches it has no reason recorded.

             refused = outcome.ok ? 'granted' : outcome.reason;

         }

         return Object.hasOwn(overrides, 'approvedCostUsd') ? req : { ...req, approvedCostUsd: await approvedFigure(req) };
       },
       state: async () => (await open()).readRunState(runId),
     };
     return body(rig);
   });
 }

 class ScriptedDriver implements Driver {
   readonly id = 'scripted';
   readonly contractVersion = '1';
   readonly requests: TaskRequest[] = [];

   }

     return { subagents: false, hooks: false, mcp: false, parallelism: 1, computerUse: false, steering: false, stablePrefixCaching: false };

     };
   }

     return Promise.resolve({
       taskId: req.taskId, claim: { narrative: this.narrative, filesChanged: [] }, events: [],
       usage: { inputTokens: 0, outputTokens: 0, cacheReadTokens: 0, cacheWriteTokens: 0, costUsd: 0, wallClockMs: 0 },
       model: this.resolveModel(req.tier), contractVersion: this.contractVersion,
     });
   }
   cancel(_taskId: TaskId): Promise<void> {
     return Promise.resolve();
   }
   emitArtifacts(_roles: CompiledRole[], _targetDir: string): Promise<void> {
     return Promise.resolve();
   }

     // Nothing fires.
   }

         const approved = await fetch(`${address}/runs/${runId}/approve`, { method: 'POST', headers, body: JSON.stringify({ key: 'integrate:1' }) });
         if (approved.status !== 200) throw new Error(`I4 control: approve answered ${String(approved.status)}`);
         const done = await settled(runId);
         if (done.standing.standing !== 'passed' || !done.state.approvals.some((g) => g.approvedBy === 'r9-principal')) {
           throw new Error(`I4 control: the run did not pass on the principal's approval: ${JSON.stringify(done.standing)}`);
         }
       } finally {
         await server.close();
       }
       if (specs.length === 0 || driver.requests.length === 0 || reviewer.requests.length === 0) throw new Error('I4 control: no sandbox was provisioned or no seat ran');
       if (JSON.stringify(specs).includes(LOCAL_TOKEN)) throw new Error('I4: a sandbox spec carries the local token');
       if (JSON.stringify([...driver.requests, ...reviewer.requests]).includes(LOCAL_TOKEN)) throw new Error('I4: a task request carries the local token');
       let stored = '';
       for (const entry of await readdir(rig.dirs.store, { recursive: true, withFileTypes: true })) {
         // A file that cannot be read fails the assertion: one skipped is one the token could hide in (codex-4).
         if (entry.isFile()) stored += await readFile(join(entry.parentPath, entry.name), 'utf8');
       }
       if (stored.includes(LOCAL_TOKEN)) throw new Error('I4: the Vault holds the local token');
     });
   },
 });

 export interface Driver {
   readonly id: string;
   readonly contractVersion: string;
   provenanceId(): string;                       // stamped onto every evidence artifact
   capabilities(): DriverCapabilities;
   /**
    * Every tool this driver can offer a task, named as policy grants it.
    *
    * The inventory half of `validateToolGrants` (I4, D-P3-04): the engine has
    * refused a grant outside the inventory since P3, and until a driver could
    * name its tools nothing in the repository could produce one, so a policy
    * could grant a tool no driver exposes and nothing noticed. Feature flags
    * are not that list — `DriverCapabilities` says whether MCP works, not which
    * tools exist.
    *
    * A driver that offers nothing returns the empty list, and every grant is
    * then refused. Empty never means allow-all.
    */
   declaredTools(): readonly string[];
   resolveModel(tier: ModelTier): ModelIdentity;
   /**
    * The model relay a sandbox this driver runs in must be provisioned with,
    * less its budget: the budget is the task's, and whoever provisions the
    * sandbox adds it (A-I1a-02, D-P12-05). `null` for a driver that calls no
    * model through a relay. Its calls are unmetered, so the line runs no task
    * on it above L1.
+   *
+   * `tools` is the call's granted tools, as policy names them. The relay's
+   * `serverTools` is derived from them and from nothing else: a tool the API
+   * runs itself is forwarded only when the grant holds the tool that uses it
+   * (I4, A-R56-01).
    */

+  relayRequest(tools: readonly string[]): Omit<RelaySpec, 'budget'> | null;
   runTask(req: TaskRequest): Promise<TaskResult>;
   /** Runs `role` as a child task under `req`'s grants: the role replaces `req.role`, and nothing else does. */
   spawnSubagent?(role: CompiledRole, req: TaskRequest): Promise<TaskResult>;
   steer?(taskId: TaskId, message: string): Promise<void>;   // runtime-only; never a human channel
   cancel(taskId: TaskId): Promise<void>;
   emitArtifacts(roles: CompiledRole[], targetDir: string): Promise<void>;
   on(kind: DriverEvent['kind'], handler: (e: DriverEvent) => void): void;
 }

   }
 
   /**
    * Runs the task under `req.role`, rendered as the agent definition the
    * session runs as (D-A-RC-03). The definition is on the argv, never a file in
    * the workspace, which the agent being judged can write (I3); and it carries
    * the instructions alone, so the tools and the model stay the request's (I4).
    */
   runTask(req: TaskRequest): Promise<TaskResult> {
     return this.#serialized(req.sandbox, () => this.#runTask(req, req.role));
   }
 
   /**
    * Runs `role` as a child task under the same grants the parent request
    * carries, and returns its own `TaskResult`.
    *
    * `--agent` rather than hoping the model reaches for the `Task` tool: a
    * capability that depends on the model choosing to exercise it is not a
    * capability the driver can be held to. The role definition is passed on the
    * same argv, so a subagent is a session this driver configured rather than
    * one the workspace supplied (I3).
    */
   spawnSubagent(role: CompiledRole, req: TaskRequest): Promise<TaskResult> {
     return this.#serialized(req.sandbox, () => this.#runTask(req, role));
   }
 
   /**
    * One invocation per sandbox at a time, and no more than `PARALLELISM`
    * across sandboxes. A task waiting for its own sandbox holds no slot of the
    * bound, so it never keeps a task on another sandbox waiting.
    */
   async #serialized<T>(handle: SandboxHandle, body: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {
     // Checked again after every wait: the call waited for may have been succeeded by another follower
     // that woke first, and the handle is claimed with no await between the check and the claim.
     for (let held = this.#inFlight.get(handle); held !== undefined; held = this.#inFlight.get(handle)) {
       await held.catch(() => undefined);
     }
     const running = this.#bounded(body);
     this.#inFlight.set(handle, running);
     try {
       return await running;
     } finally {
       if (this.#inFlight.get(handle) === running) this.#inFlight.delete(handle);
     }
   }
 
   /** Runs `body` once fewer than `PARALLELISM` invocations are running, in the order callers arrived. */
   async #bounded<T>(body: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {
     if (this.#running >= PARALLELISM) await new Promise<void>((admit) => this.#waiting.push(admit));
     else this.#running += 1;
     try {
       return await body();
     } finally {
       // A slot passes straight to the next caller waiting, so the count never dips to let a newcomer jump the queue.
       const next = this.#waiting.shift();
       if (next === undefined) this.#running -= 1;
       else next();
     }
   }
 
   async #runTask(req: TaskRequest, role: CompiledRole): Promise<TaskResult> {
     this.#refuseUngrantedTools(req);
     const identity = this.resolveModel(req.tier);
     const servers = this.#mcpConfigFor(req);
     const configPath = await this.#writeMcpConfig(req, servers);
     const ungranted = await this.#ungrantedMcpTools(req, servers, configPath);
     const invocation = this.#invocationFor(req, identity, role, configPath, ungranted);
 
     const exec = await this.#provider.exec(req.sandbox, invocation.argv, { env: invocation.env });
     const stream = parseStream(exec.stdout);
     const init = this.#refuseUnusableStream(req, exec.exitCode, exec.stderr, stream);
     const events = eventsFrom(stream);
     for (const event of events) this.#emit(event);
     this.#sessions.set(req.taskId, init);
 
     return {
       taskId: req.taskId,
       claim: claimFrom(stream),
       events,
       usage: usageFrom(stream, exec.durationMs),
       // The identity the task actually ran under: the alias this driver asked
       // for, replaced by the model the session says it resolved to. `family`
       // is never touched by that replacement (I6).
       model: init.model === '' ? identity : { ...identity, model: init.model },
       contractVersion: this.contractVersion,
     };
   }
 
   /**
    * `steer` is deliberately absent, because `capabilities().steering` is
    * false. A method that threw would be a capability declared false and
    * present, which is the direction the conformance assertion refuses.
    */
 
   /**
    * Forgets a task's session. The container belongs to the run rather than to
    * this driver, so nothing here destroys it: a driver that tore down a
    * sandbox it did not provision would end every other task sharing it.
    */
   cancel(taskId: TaskId): Promise<void> {
     this.#sessions.delete(taskId);
     return Promise.resolve();
   }
 
   /**
    * Renders roles into the CLI's own artifact format under `targetDir`.
    *
    * Written through the sandbox like everything else, so the artifacts land on
    * the mount table rather than on the host. `CompiledRole.instructions` is
    * rendered verbatim: what the instructions should say is the compiler's
    * (M2), and this driver's job is the file format.
    */
   async emitArtifacts(roles: CompiledRole[], targetDir: string): Promise<void> {
     if (roles.length === 0) refuse('capability', 'emitArtifacts was given no roles; there is nothing to write');
     const handle = this.#artifactSandbox;
     if (handle === undefined) {
       refuse('provider', 'emitArtifacts has no sandbox to write into; call useSandbox() with the handle the run provisioned (I1)');
     }
     for (const file of artifactFiles(roles, targetDir)) {
       const written = await this.#provider.exec(handle, writeFileArgv(file.path), { env: { [ARTIFACT_CONTENT]: file.content } });
       if (written.exitCode !== 0) {
         refuse('invocation', `writing ${file.path} inside the sandbox failed with exit code ${String(written.exitCode)}: ${written.stderr.trim()}`);
       }
     }
   }
 
   on(kind: DriverEvent['kind'], handler: (e: DriverEvent) => void): void {
     const existing = this.#handlers.get(kind);
     if (existing === undefined) this.#handlers.set(kind, [handler]);
     else existing.push(handler);
   }
 
   /** Names the sandbox `emitArtifacts` writes into, for a caller emitting before any task has run. */
   useSandbox(handle: SandboxHandle): void {
     this.#artifactSandbox = handle;
   }
 
   /** What the CLI reported about a task's session, once one has run. */
   sessionFor(taskId: TaskId): SessionInit | undefined {
     return this.#sessions.get(taskId);
   }
 
   /**
    * I4: a granted tool this driver does not declare is refused before the task
    * starts. `validateToolGrants` applies this rule to a policy; this applies it
    * to the grant that actually reaches the CLI, so a caller that bypassed the
    * engine does not get a wider session than policy would have allowed.
    */
   #refuseUngrantedTools(req: TaskRequest): void {
     const declared = new Set(DECLARED_TOOLS);
     // MCP tools are not the driver's to declare — they belong to whichever
     // server a request configures — so they are checked against the server map
     // instead, by `#mcpConfigFor`.
     const undeclared = req.tools.filter((tool) => mcpServerOf(tool) === undefined && !declared.has(tool));
     if (undeclared.length > 0) {
       refuse(
         'grant',
         `the task grants ${undeclared.map((t) => `'${t}'`).join(', ')}, which this driver does not declare. ` +
           'The task was not started: a grant this driver cannot honour is a policy that means something other than what it says (I4).',
       );
     }
   }
 
   /**
    * The MCP servers this request's grants name, and nothing else.
    *
    * A grant naming a server the driver does not hold is refused rather than
    * dropped: the CLI silently ignores a tool name it does not know, so a
    * policy could grant `mcp__missing__tool`, get a session without it, and
    * read as though the grant had taken effect (I4, I5).
    */
   #mcpConfigFor(req: TaskRequest): Record<string, McpServerConfig> {
     const wanted: Record<string, McpServerConfig> = {};
     for (const tool of req.tools) {
       const server = mcpServerOf(tool);
       if (server === undefined) continue;
       const config = this.#mcpServers[server];
       if (config === undefined) {
         refuse('grant', `the task grants '${tool}', but this driver holds no MCP server named '${server}'`);
       }
       wanted[server] = config;
     }
     return wanted;
   }
 
   /**
    * Every MCP tool the request's servers offer that the request did not grant.
    *
    * `--tools` governs the built-in set only: an MCP server contributes its
    * whole tool list to a session, so granting `mcp__s__a` on a server that also
    * offers `mcp__s__b` produces a session holding both. That is a tool
    * available to the model that policy did not grant, which is precisely what
    * I4 forbids, and it is not something a narrower grant can fix because the
    * names come from the server rather than from the driver.
    *
    * What does fix it is naming them: `--disallowedTools mcp__s__b` removes it
    * from the session entirely, not merely from what the model may use. So the
    * driver asks the CLI what the servers offer before it runs the task. The
    * inspection starts a session and reads the list it prints, which costs a
    * process and no tokens — the CLI prints its session before it calls
    * anything, and this invocation is given no tools and is bounded so it
    * cannot sit there retrying.
    *
    * Only when the request grants an MCP tool at all. A task with no MCP grant
    * configures no server, so there is nothing to inspect and nothing to hide.
    */
   async #ungrantedMcpTools(req: TaskRequest, servers: Record<string, McpServerConfig>, configPath: string | undefined): Promise<string[]> {
     if (configPath === undefined || Object.keys(servers).length === 0) return [];
     const inspected = await this.#provider.exec(
       req.sandbox,
       [
         'sh',
         '-c',
         `mkdir -p "$HOME" && cd ${shellQuote(this.#workdir)} && exec timeout ${String(INSPECT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS)} "$@"`,
         'driver',
         'claude',
         '--print', 'the session is read before any turn',
         '--output-format', 'stream-json',
         '--verbose',
         '--tools', '',
         '--setting-sources', '',
         '--strict-mcp-config',
         '--mcp-config', configPath,
         '--permission-mode', 'bypassPermissions',
         '--permission-prompts', 'none',
         '--no-session-persistence',
       ],
       { env: CLI_KEY_ENVIRONMENT },
     );
     const init = parseStream(inspected.stdout).init;
     if (init === undefined) {
       refuse(
         'invocation',
         `the MCP servers ${Object.keys(servers).join(', ')} could not be inspected, so the tools they offer beyond the grant are unknown. ` +
           'The task was not started: running it would mean offering the model tools nobody can enumerate (I4, I5).',
       );
     }
     // An `init` line is not an enumeration. A server that came up failed, or
     // was still connecting, contributes no tools to the list, so a disallow
     // list computed from it would be short by exactly the tools that server
     // offers -- and the task would then run holding them. Every configured
     // server has to be connected, and every granted tool has to be present,
     // before the absence of a name means anything (I5).
     const unconnected = init.mcpServers.filter((server) => server.status !== 'connected');
     if (unconnected.length > 0) {
       refuse(
         'invocation',
         `MCP server(s) ${unconnected.map((server) => `${server.name} (${server.status})`).join(', ')} did not connect during inspection, ` +
           'so what they offer is unknown and the tools to disallow cannot be computed',
       );
     }
     const enumerated = new Set(init.tools);
     const absent = req.tools.filter((tool) => mcpServerOf(tool) !== undefined && !enumerated.has(tool));
     if (absent.length > 0) {
       refuse(
         'grant',
         `the inspection did not find granted MCP tool(s) ${absent.join(', ')}; the servers offer a different set than the request names`,
       );
     }
     const granted = new Set(req.tools);
     return init.tools.filter((tool) => mcpServerOf(tool) !== undefined && !granted.has(tool));
   }
 
   /**
    * Writes the MCP configuration into the container and returns its path, or
    * `undefined` when the request granted no MCP tool.
    *
    * A file rather than a JSON string on the argv. The CLI takes `--mcp-config`
    * as "JSON files or strings (space-separated)", and a server definition that
    * carries a command with arguments has spaces in it, so passing one inline
    * produced a session with no MCP tools at all — and the model, asked to use
    * one, invented a tool list rather than saying it had none. A path has no
    * spaces and is what the documented file form expects.
    *
    * It lives in the container's own filesystem rather than the workspace mount,
    * which keeps it out of the tree a task works on and out of any diff. That is
    * all the location buys. The driver and the agent's own commands run as the
    * same user, so a process the agent started can rewrite this file between the
    * inspection and the run. What makes that pointless is not where the file
    * sits but `#refuseSessionUnlikeGrant`, which compares the session the CLI
    * actually started against the grant and refuses a tool nobody granted
    * however it arrived.
    *
    * Nothing secret is in it, and `McpServerConfig` has no field that could
    * carry one: a server environment was removed from the type rather than left
    * as a place a caller might put a token the agent could then read.
    */
   async #writeMcpConfig(req: TaskRequest, servers: Record<string, McpServerConfig>): Promise<string | undefined> {
     if (Object.keys(servers).length === 0) return undefined;
     const path = `/tmp/mcp-${sessionIdFor(req.taskId)}.json`;
     const written = await this.#provider.exec(req.sandbox, writeFileArgv(path), {
       env: { [ARTIFACT_CONTENT]: JSON.stringify({ mcpServers: servers }) },
     });
     if (written.exitCode !== 0) {
       refuse('invocation', `the MCP configuration could not be written to ${path}: ${written.stderr.trim()}`);
     }
     return path;
   }
 
   #invocationFor(
     req: TaskRequest,
     identity: ModelIdentity,
     role: CompiledRole,
     configPath: string | undefined,
     ungrantedMcpTools: readonly string[],
   ): Invocation {
     // `--tools` and not `--allowedTools`: the first decides which tools exist
     // in the session at all, the second decides which uses need approval. I4
     // is about availability — a tool outside the grant must be unavailable to
     // the model, not merely unused — so the narrower flag is the correct one.
     // An empty grant produces `--tools ''`, which is a session with no tools:
     // default deny, spelled the way the CLI spells it.
     const cli = [
       'claude',
       '--print', req.variableSuffix,
       '--output-format', 'stream-json',
       '--verbose',
       '--model', identity.model,
       // The cacheable half: the role, then the granted context. Appended rather
       // than replacing the default prompt, so the session still behaves like
       // the CLI it is, and the dynamic sections that change between tasks —
       // cwd, git status, env — are moved out of the prefix, so two tasks under
       // one role present the same prefix and the second reads it from cache
       // instead of writing it again. The role is its instructions and nothing
       // else, so nothing beside them can widen the tools or change the model
       // (I4), and an argv element cannot go unloaded the way an agent
       // definition could (D-A-RC-03, D-R20-14).
       '--append-system-prompt', `${role.instructions}\n\n${req.stablePrefix}`,
       '--exclude-dynamic-system-prompt-sections',
       '--tools', req.tools.filter((tool) => mcpServerOf(tool) === undefined).join(','),
       // Nothing outside this argv configures the session: no user, project, or
       // local settings file, and no MCP server the request did not name. A
       // session configured by a settings file in the workspace would be
       // configured by the thing it is being judged on (I3).
       '--setting-sources', '',
       '--strict-mcp-config',
       // The container is the sandbox. A permission prompt has nobody to answer
       // it, and `none` denies anything that would prompt rather than stalling
       // until the wall-clock budget kills the container.
       '--permission-mode', 'bypassPermissions',
       '--permission-prompts', 'none',
       '--no-session-persistence',
       '--session-id', sessionIdFor(req.taskId),
       '--include-hook-events',
     ];
     if (configPath !== undefined) cli.push('--mcp-config', configPath);
     // Named one by one, because a server-wide pattern removes the granted tools
     // with the rest and a later `--allowedTools` does not bring them back.
     if (ungrantedMcpTools.length > 0) cli.push('--disallowedTools', ungrantedMcpTools.join(','));
     if (this.#settingsPath !== undefined) cli.push('--settings', this.#settingsPath);
     // `exec "$@"` runs the CLI as the process itself, with every argument an
     // argv element the shell never re-reads. Only the working directory is
     // interpolated, and it is quoted.
     return {
       argv: ['sh', '-c', `mkdir -p "$HOME" && cd ${shellQuote(this.#workdir)} && exec "$@"`, 'driver', ...cli],
       env: environmentFor(identity.model),
     };
   }
 
   /**
    * Fail closed on anything that means the task did not run (I5), and return
    * the session that did.
    *
    * A session the CLI never started, a credential from a source this driver
    * did not arrange, a missing result, and an API status the CLI gave up on
    * are each a refusal. None of them is a `TaskResult` with a sad narrative in
    * it: a `TaskResult` says a task ran, and the runtime derives status from
    * evidence that assumes one did.
    */
   #refuseUnusableStream(req: TaskRequest, exitCode: number, stderr: string, stream: ParsedStream): NonNullable<ParsedStream['init']> {
     const init = stream.init;
     if (init === undefined) {
       const first = stream.unreadable[0];
       refuse(
         'invocation',
         `the CLI produced no session for task ${req.taskId} (exit code ${String(exitCode)})` +
           (first === undefined ? '' : `; the first unreadable line was ${JSON.stringify(first.slice(0, 200))}`) +
           (stderr.trim() === '' ? '' : `; stderr: ${stderr.trim().slice(0, 400)}`),
       );
     }
     if (init.apiKeySource !== KEY_VARIABLE) {
       refuse(
         'credential',
         `the session authenticated from '${init.apiKeySource}' rather than ${KEY_VARIABLE}; ` +
           'a key this driver did not supply is one it cannot account for, and one the CLI found is a credential inside the sandbox',
       );
     }
     const result = stream.result;
     if (result === undefined) {
       refuse('invocation', `the CLI ended without a result for task ${req.taskId} (exit code ${String(exitCode)})`);
     }
     if (result.apiErrorStatus !== undefined) {
       refuse(
         'invocation',
         `the model was not reached for task ${req.taskId}: the API answered ${String(result.apiErrorStatus)} and the CLI stopped retrying` +
           (result.narrative.trim() === '' ? '' : `; the CLI reported ${JSON.stringify(result.narrative.trim().slice(0, 400))}`),
       );
     }
     if (result.isError && result.numTurns === 0) {
       refuse('invocation', `the CLI took no turn for task ${req.taskId}: ${result.terminalReason ?? 'no reason given'}`);
     }
     // Nothing was refused mid-task. Under `bypassPermissions` there is nothing
     // to prompt and therefore nothing to deny, so a denial means the task ran
     // with less than it was granted, and the result describes work done under
     // conditions the request did not ask for (I5).
     if (result.permissionDenials.length > 0) {
       refuse(
         'grant',
         `task ${req.taskId} had ${String(result.permissionDenials.length)} tool use(s) denied ` +
           `(${result.permissionDenials.join(', ')}), so it did not run under the capabilities it was granted`,
       );
     }
     this.#refuseSessionUnlikeGrant(req, init);
     return init;
   }
 
   /**
    * The session the CLI actually started must offer exactly what the request
    * granted: no more, and no less.
    *
    * Everything else in this driver is an argument it hopes the CLI honours --
    * `--tools`, `--disallowedTools`, `--mcp-config`, `--strict-mcp-config`. This
    * is the one place that checks. The CLI prints what the session came up with
    * before it takes a turn, so the check costs nothing, and it is the backstop
    * for every assumption those flags rest on. Two of those assumptions were
    * already wrong before anyone read the session report: an inline MCP
    * configuration silently produced no servers at all, and a granted server
    * contributed tools nobody granted.
    *
    * Both directions refuse. A tool the session holds and the request did not
    * grant is a capability arriving from somewhere other than policy, which is
    * the whole of I4. A tool the request granted and the session does not hold
    * is a grant that did not take effect, and a task that runs anyway produces a
    * result describing work done under conditions nobody asked for, which is the
    * silent degrade I5 refuses.
    */
   #refuseSessionUnlikeGrant(req: TaskRequest, init: SessionInit): void {
     const granted = new Set(req.tools);
     const offered = new Set(init.tools);
     const ungranted = [...offered].filter((tool) => !granted.has(tool)).sort((a, b) => a.localeCompare(b));
     const missing = [...granted].filter((tool) => !offered.has(tool)).sort((a, b) => a.localeCompare(b));
     if (ungranted.length > 0) {
       refuse(
         'grant',
         `the session for task ${req.taskId} offers ${ungranted.map((t) => `'${t}'`).join(', ')}, which the request did not grant. ` +
           'A capability the model can reach and policy did not grant is the failure I4 exists to prevent.',
       );
     }
     if (missing.length > 0) {
       refuse(
         'grant',
         `the session for task ${req.taskId} does not offer ${missing.map((t) => `'${t}'`).join(', ')}, which the request granted. ` +
           'The grant did not take effect, and a task run without it would describe work done under conditions nobody asked for (I5).',
       );
     }
     const configured = new Set(Object.keys(this.#mcpServersFor(req)));
     const loaded = init.mcpServers.map((server) => server.name);
     const unexpected = loaded.filter((name) => !configured.has(name));
     if (unexpected.length > 0) {
       refuse(
         'grant',
         `the session for task ${req.taskId} loaded MCP server(s) ${unexpected.join(', ')}, which this driver did not configure`,
       );
     }
     const broken = init.mcpServers.filter((server) => server.status !== 'connected');
     if (broken.length > 0) {
       refuse(
         'invocation',
         `the session for task ${req.taskId} has MCP server(s) ${broken.map((b) => `${b.name} (${b.status})`).join(', ')} that are not connected; ` +
           'the granted tools were not reachable',
       );
     }
   }
 
   /** The servers this request's grants name. `#mcpConfigFor` refuses an unheld one; this one is for a grant already validated. */
   #mcpServersFor(req: TaskRequest): Record<string, McpServerConfig> {
     const wanted: Record<string, McpServerConfig> = {};
     for (const tool of req.tools) {
       const server = mcpServerOf(tool);
       if (server === undefined) continue;
       const config = this.#mcpServers[server];
       if (config !== undefined) wanted[server] = config;
     }
     return wanted;
   }
 
   #emit(event: DriverEvent): void {
     for (const handler of this.#handlers.get(event.kind) ?? []) handler(event);
   }
 }
 
 /**
  * The event stream, built from what the CLI reported rather than from what the
  * model said about itself.
  *
  * These are observations of the invocation, and the driver sees only what the
  * CLI emits: a write the CLI did not report is a write this list does not
  * have. That is exactly why P6 collects the diff itself and why `filesChanged`
  * lives on `AgentClaim` (D-P4-01).
  */
